modern computing.

Sensors are devices or components that detect
changes in ambient conditions and convert
these changes into measurable or usable
signals.
ixfõol hkq wjg ;;a;ajfha fjkialï
y÷kdf.k fuu fjkialï ueksh yels fyda Ndú;
l< yels ix{d njg mßj¾;kh lrk Wmdx. fyda
ixrpl fõ'

They often act as the interface between the
physical world and electronic systems. Sensors
provide critical information for monitoring,
control and automation purposes.
tAjd fndfyda úg fN!;sl f,dalh iy bf,lafg%dksl
moaO;s w;r w;=re uqyqK; f,i l%shd lrhs'

i|yd ;SrKd;aul f;dr;=re ixfõol u.ska
imhhs'

The primary function of sensors is to sense and
measure physical phenomena such as
temperature, pressure, light, movement,
proximity, sound, humidity and many others.
ixfõolj, uQ,sl ld¾hh jkafka WIaK;ajh"
mSvkh" wdf,dalh" p,kh" ióm;ajh" Yíoh"

oekSu iy uekSuhs'

They can detect these phenomena by various
methods including electrical, mechanical,
optical, chemical or biological principles.

the quantity being measured.

Plasma Screen                                            Plasma screens work by utilizing small cells
Plasma ;srh                                              filled with ionized gases. When an electrical
                                                         current is applied to these gases, they emit
                                                         ultraviolet light.
                                                         ma,diaud ;sr l%shd lrkafka whkSlD; jdhq j,ska
                                                         msreKq l=vd ffi, Ndú;d lrñks' fuu jdhqj,g

                                                         lrhs'

                                                         This UV light then interacts with phosphors
                                                         coated on the walls of each cell, causing them
                                                         to emit visible light, which forms the images
                                                         displayed on the screen.

2) Non Impact Printers
>Ügkh fkdjk uqøK hka;%                                      1.   Direct Thermal

Non-impact printers are printing devices that do

device in which data is electronically stored.          that use integrated circuit assemblies to store

Parallel Computing                                        For an example, a single CPU adds 1 million
iudka;r mß.Kkh                                            numbers sequentially, whereas 1,000 CPUs can
                                                          divide the work among themselves, with each
                                                          CPU handling about 1,000 numbers at the same
                                                          time.
                                                          WodyrKhla f,i" ;ks CPU tllg ixLHd
                                                          ñ,shkhla tlsfkl ms<sfj,ska tl;= lsÍug isÿ jk
                                                          w;r" CPU 1,000la we;s úg tu ld¾hh Tjqka w;r
                                                          fnodf.k" tla CPU tlla uÕska tlu wjia:djl§
                                                          ixLHd 1,000 ne.ska yiqrejkq ,nhs'

                                                          Applications
In parallel computing, tasks are processed                fh§ï
simultaneously across multiple computing                  • Modeling complex scientific systems like
devices, enhancing speed and efficiency.                     weather patterns
iudka;r mß.Kkfha §" ld¾hhka nyq mß.Kl                        ld,.=K rgd jeks ixlS¾K úoHd;aul moaO;s
Wmdx. yryd tljr ilikq ,efí" fõ.h iy                          wdlD;s ks¾udKh
ld¾hlaIu;dj jeä lrhs'                                     • Processing and analyzing massive datasets
                                                             for insights
By dividing the workload, parallel computing                 ;SlaIK nqoaêh i|yd oejeka; o;a; lÜg,
significantly reduces the overall time to solve              ieliSu iy úYaf,aIKh lsÍu
complex problems.                                         • Rendering realistic images and animations.
ld¾h Ndrh fn§u u.ska" iudka;r mß.Kkh                         h:d¾:jd§ rEm iy iÔúlrK úoeyqïlrKh'
ixlS¾K .eg¿ úi£u i|yd .; jk iuia;                         • Encoding and decoding high-resolution

Computer Speed
mß.Kl fõ.h

The clock speed of the computer processor (CPU)             In modern computers, clock speed is usually
is the key factor affecting the processing speed of         measured in Megahertz (MHz) or Gigahertz (GHz).
a computer.                                                 kùk mß.Klj, >áld fõ.h idudkHfhka ukskq
mß.Kl ilikfha (CPU) >áld fõ.h mß.Klhl                       ,nkafka Megahertz (MHz) fyda Gigahertz (GHz)
ilik fõ.hg n,mdk m%Odk idOlh fõ'                            j,sks'

The clock speed is measured in Hertz (Hz), which            1 MHz means 1 million cycles per second.
means "cycles per second". It refers to the number          1 MHz hkq ;;amrhlg pl% ñ,shk 1 ls'
of operations the CPU can perform in one second.
                                                            1 GHz means 1 billion cycles per second.
>áld fõ.h Hertz (Hz) j,ska ukskq ,efí" tys
                                                            1 GHz hkq ;;amrhlg pl% ì,shk 1 ls'

If the socket type is different, the CPU cannot
be installed.
fidlÜ j¾.h fjkia kï" ilikh iú lsÍug                     TDP refers to the amount of heat a CPU is
fkdyels fõ'                                             designed to produce during normal operation,
                                                        measured in watts (W).
Socket types like LGA, PGA, and BGA are                 TDP uÕska woyia jkafka ilikhla idudkH
different ways a CPU connects to a                      l%shdldÍ;ajfha§ msg lsÍug ie,iqï lr we;s ;dm

Memory Hierarchy                                        Reduce Average Access Time
u;l Oqrdj,sh                                            idudkH m%fõY ld,h wju lsÍu
                                                        Frequently used data is stored in the fastest
                                                        memory levels closest to the processor, making
                                                        the overall average memory access speed much
                                                        faster and smoother for daily tasks.
                                                        ks;r ks;r Ndú; jk o;a;" ilikhg
                                                        wdikakfhkau we;s fõ.j;au u;l ia:rhkays
                                                        .nvd lr ;nhs' fï fya;=fjka tÈfkod ld¾hhka
                                                        i|yd u;lhg m%fõY ùfï iuia; idudkH fõ.h
                                                        fnfyúka jeä jk w;r th iqugj isÿfõ'

                                                        Exploit Temporal Locality
                                                        ld,Sk ia:dkSh;djfhka m%fhdack .ekSu
                                                        Recently accessed data is likely to be needed
                                                        again soon, so keeping it in fast memory speeds
                                                        up future requests.
                                                        uE;l§ m%fõY jQ o;a; kej;;a flá l,lska
                                                        wjYH ùfï by< iïNdú;djla we;' tneúka tu

                                                        bÈß o;a; b,a,Sï jvd;a fõ.j;a lrhs'
Why is Memory Hierarchy Required?
u;l Oqrdj,shla wjYH jkafka wehs?                        Reduce Cost
                                                        msßjeh wvq lsÍu
No Perfect Memory Technology                            Building a computer entirely from registers
mßmQ¾K u;l ;dlaIKhla fkdue;s ùu                         would cost millions and provide tiny capacity,
computers combine multiple types like tiny,             whereas building it solely from HDDs would be
ultra-fast registers with large, slow HDDs to           extremely slow even though it provides a huge
balance speed and capacity.                             storage capacity.
fõ.h iy Odß;dj w;r iunr;djla mj;ajd                     mß.Klhla iïmQ¾Kfhkau frðia;r muKla
.ekSu i|yd" mß.Kl ;=< b;d l=vd fukau                    Ndú;fhka ks¾udKh l<fyd;a tA i|yd ñ,shk

tfy;a ukao.dó oDV ;eá jeks úúO j¾.fha
u;lhka tlsfkl tAldnoaOj Ndú; lrhs'                      fõ' wfkla w;g" th iïmQ¾Kfhkau HDDs
                                                        muKla Ndú;fhka ks¾udKh l<fyd;a thska úYd,

                       msßjeh

             Fast                                       without sacrificing performance.

Reduce CPU Waiting Time                                 .ekSu i|ydh.
CPU /£ isàfï ld,h wju lsÍu

memory; integrating ultra-fast Cache bridges            Odß;dj jeä lsÍu

the CPU spends waiting to retrieve necessary            scale to large sizes, so hierarchy integrates
data.                                                   bigger, cheaper storage to handle massive

 Capacity                   Lowest         Low                 Medium                    Highest

 Cost per bit               Highest        High                Medium                    Lowest

This flip-flop holds its state (0 or 1) as long as
power is supplied, without needing any refresh.
fuu *a,sma-*af,dma tl" úÿ,s n,h imhk ;dla l,a
lsisÿ kej; m%fndaOu;a lsÍulska ^refresh& f;drj
tys ;;a;ajh ^0 fyda 1& r|jd .kS'

Each bit in SRAM is stored in a bistable latch a
circuit with two stable states. The latch holds its
state without any intervention. As long as the
power remains, the data stays. There are no
capacitors, no charge leakage, and no need to
periodically rewrite the data.
